Price notes and arrow markers take two points. Positions place entry, target and stop in one click, with independent editable anchors. Extensions project the price leg from the third anchor and trend-based time projects its bar interval there.
- Rotated rectangles use a sloping base and perpendicular width, Curve has a placed quadratic control, Double curve has two placed cubic controls, arcs pass through a third control, and pitchfan rays pass through Fibonacci fractions of the second-to-third anchor span. Table, Image, X post, Idea and Ghost feed stay loadable but are removed from tool menus until their content is implemented. Migration: new Curve and Double curve placements collect three and four anchors. Older saved placements still load: rotated rectangles gain their axis-aligned corner, double curves gain endpoint controls, wedges and pitchfans gain a boundary at the second time and first price, and one-point notes and markers gain a coincident second anchor. The added controls can be dragged after loading.

- Fibonacci speed resistance fans include the time fan, ratio grid and quarter arcs in the anchor direction, and selection follows them. A three-anchor Fib wedge draws circular ratio arcs between its two trend rays, using the first trend radius and the signed shorter sweep.

- Measuring tools count signed ticks and pips from `Instrument::tick_size` and the new optional `pip_size` (`with_pip_size`). Unknown, non-positive and non-finite sizes are omitted. `Chart` passes its host or feed instrument to drawings. Migration: `ChartView` literals set `instrument` (`None` when unknown); `Instrument` literals set `pip_size: None` or use `Instrument::new`.

- Gann fans follow the signed anchor direction and retain their steep rays when they leave through the pane's top or bottom. Fixed Gann squares keep their anchor-defined price-per-bar ratio through zoom. `Drawing::gann` (`GannConfig`) sets independent price and time grid fractions on boxes and squares; levels count from the first anchor. Migration: `Drawing` struct literals set `gann`, or use `Drawing::new`. Saved grid and pitchfork settings reject non-finite values.

- Inside pitchforks use the first leg midpoint and second anchor as their tines, with the first tine passing through the third anchor and the median halfway between the tines. All fork rays follow their direction, including leftward and vertical forks.

- ABCD, XABCD and Cypher label every price ratio on its comparison chord, including C's retracement and D's placement. Undefined ratios on flat legs are omitted.

- Session breaks and previous-session closes follow exchange windows and holidays, with chart-zone calendar boundaries when no exchange is supplied. `DailySessionProvider` now honors its configured break time, and its exchange preset follows daylight-saving changes. Migration: `DailySessionProvider` struct literals need `time_zone` or `..Default::default()`; `new` still takes UTC hours.

- Time-scale clock, seconds and tick visibility flags now reach axis and crosshair rendering, and `ChartBuilder::options_chart` hides its time axis. Migration: `time_visible` defaults to `true` to preserve existing labels; `false` shows dates alone.

- Floating and tracking tooltip times use the same chart-zone formatter as the crosshair, including daylight-saving changes. Exchange display mode reads the instrument zone when supplied. `Chart::format_time` gives host readouts the same format and source-bar precision on every chart type, and the demo Data Window uses it. Floating readouts paint above the legend so their time row remains visible.

- Migration for standalone renderers: pass the chart-zone `TimeFormatter` and the bar duration in seconds as the final two arguments to `render_tooltip_with_options`. Pass `ticks_visible` before `slot` to `render_time_labels`; `false` preserves the previous tick-free axis.

- Applying the demo's time-zone setting updates chart readouts and the bottom clock without resetting pan or zoom. Reopening settings retains the zone selected from either control.

- Daily axis marks use fixed calendar anchors for two-day and weekly intervals, keeping their dates stable while panning. Month boundaries remain visible between day strides and on the first of the displayed month at medium widths and wide time ranges, including across daylight-saving changes.

- Horizontal arrow keys keep keyboard focus on the chart and pan on every press. Tab still moves to the next control.

- `Chart::visible_bars` now applies the requested initial bar spacing on the first frame, matching `set_visible_bars`.

- Chart settings read back current status and scale controls before opening. Choosing log or percentage scale clears the other mode; legend volume remains independent of the histogram. Custom price-line tags and axis gestures follow left placement, and long session tags stay inside the chart.
- Enlarged modals fit narrow windows, with scrollable settings content and tabs. Indicator results use a 32 px row pitch.
- Symbol search gives its category strip and results separate scroll identities, keeps keyboard selection under a stationary pointer and scrolls the selected result into view. ([#597](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/597))
- Opening chart settings now reads the chart's current candle, background and grid colours, so applying display controls preserves its appearance in every preset. ([#577](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/577))

- The host Trade and Publish controls now explain their trading-account and chart-export actions on hover, using the shared toolbar tooltip.

- The interval menu reserves a compact 200 px outer width until the custom form is expanded; the form retains its full input and unit controls.

- Chart settings opens on Symbol in the shared modal, with a 752 px surface, tab-specific heights, neutral sidebar selection and aligned value columns. Existing tabs retain their controls and template actions; dropdown selection remains readable.
- The indicator dialog uses an 840 px surface, a 40 px search field, a wider category column and single-line study names. Categories with no provider are omitted from the sidebar; favourite and study configuration actions are retained. Filter fields respect the host interaction height.
- Quick search uses the shared 480 px modal with a search header and close button. The demo connects its quick-search button and Ctrl/Cmd+K to the existing command palette for symbol search, indicators, settings and export. Shared dialog titles use 20 px text.
- The compare dialog uses the shared modal frame, a wide search header, a close control and a single list of 56 px symbol rows. Its configured fill remains supported through `Modal::fill`.
- Symbol search uses the shared modal frame, a full-width search row with a close button and 56 px result rows. Escape and backdrop dismissal follow the modal; symbol filtering and keyboard selection are preserved. Empty modal titles allow a search row to serve as the header.
- The Save arrow opens a compact export menu while the label keeps its existing action. Entries requiring hosted layout storage are omitted.
- Snapshot offers image download in a compact menu beneath its trigger. Opening the menu leaves capture to an explicit download choice.
- Chart-type menu rows use a single 14 px name beside each icon, neutral selection and separators between type families.
- The interval menu groups full interval names in 32 px rows with trailing favourite marks and neutral selection, retaining custom interval entry and model selection.
- Top toolbar tooltips use compact dark surfaces below their controls, with platform-specific shortcut chips for alerts. Symbol, interval and chart-type triggers now identify their actions on hover.

- The demo opens at one minute in Arrow mode with axis readouts. Synthetic sessions stay continuous, with shorter wicks, varying low ordinary volumes and occasional peaks. Volume peaks use a quarter of the plot height with a flush baseline, and candle bodies use 75% of the bar pitch. Hourly data remains available; session timestamps and adjustment inputs are unchanged.
- The in-pane legend and held values keep the chart's configured candle colours. The legend title exposes a flag and copy actions on hover. The Data Window computes change from the preceding displayed element, matching the legend on both time-based and price-based charts. `ChartState::shown` exposes the series indexed by hover and selection.
- Time axes use denser calendar marks shared with the vertical grid and semibold day, month and year labels. Round price levels use semibold labels. Minute intervals retain their unit in the toolbar, and interval picks update the trigger and selected row.
- Dark plot and panels are black, with neutral hover fills quieter than pressed fills. Volume uses muted teal and salmon at the configured theme opacity, and both themes use dotted grids. The demo preserves its rounded chart container.
- Cursor choices use 40 px rows, larger glyphs, a neutral selected row and a labelled values switch, with visible keyboard focus. Held values use a compact six-row panel. Eraser suppresses the previous cursor's trail and crosshair strokes.
- Toolbar controls, including replay, undo and redo, move into overflow before overlapping host actions at narrow widths. Demo Search, Trade and Publish keep their intended order and sizes.
- Price ticks stay on the supplied decimal precision when zoomed in. Intraday marks survive bars that start between clock boundaries, and daily charts at multi-month zoom use calendar month names and year boundaries. Hover tags remain within narrow viewports.
- Crosshair price and time tags default to black with white text, use 21 px and 25 px rows, and clamp at both horizontal edges. The price row reserves a 21 px add-action cell for the host to route with `chart::crosshair_price_action`. The default crosshair stroke is dotted; explicit colours and line styles remain supported. `chart::render_crosshair_full` and `RenderContext` let hosts draw the same readout over a combined price and volume viewport.
- Price lines use 4 px dashes with 4 px gaps, and dotted price lines use 1 px dots with 3 px gaps. Drawing line patterns keep their existing lengths. Price tags are about 17 px high at the default axis font size.
- Intraday time marks use actual session bars and the formatter's timezone for calendar boundaries, so closed hours and missing weekends take no slots and month labels name the new month. Day labels show the day number. `TimeFormatter::wall_time` supplies calendar fields and `format_crosshair` supplies a hover readout; both have defaults for existing custom formatters. Intraday hover dates include weekday, day, month, short year and time, with fractional seconds preserved through timezone conversion.
- Price ticks, price lines and crosshair price labels use the feed instrument's `price_decimals`, which clears when the feed reloads. Hosts without a feed can set `PriceScale::set_price_precision`; an unspecified precision keeps at least two decimals on price ticks. Default price marks stay at least 40 px apart. Price tags have square left corners and white text on financial colours, while pale custom fills retain dark text.

- The series legend wraps between whole OHLC fields within its supplied plot bounds, truncates long titles and paints the chart background behind text. The title uses 16 px text and the readout 13 px, with financial colours and 8 px field gaps; wide readouts stay inline.
- Price and study panes share the available height at a 3 to 1 weight per study, with one 28 point time axis below the lowest pane. A study that would be under 40 points collapses to a 24 point legend row; clicking its title expands or collapses it. Pane plots and selection handles use the main plot's horizontal bounds and clip to the allocated pane, including after a resize. ([#736](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/736))
- The bottom clock converts UTC instants to the named time zone, including daylight-saving offsets, and falls back to UTC digits and a UTC label for unknown names.
- The bottom bar uses 14 px primary text, measures its labels to align the clock and session controls at the right edge, and keeps range buttons unfilled at rest. Clicking the remembered range requests it again after panning; narrow bars keep all ranges in a menu. The date button has a 24 px icon grid beside a 22 px rule.
- The top toolbar is 38 px high with larger icons, a filled symbol pill with a semibold ticker, a plain interval and an icon-only chart type trigger. Group rules are 22 px high with compact gaps, and theme and search actions open from the menu. `TopToolbar::show_with_slots` reserves space for a host menu and right-side actions before compacting the built-in controls; alert, indicator and settings actions also move into overflow when a long symbol leaves too little room.
- The demo opens in light with labelled synthetic one-minute candles, volume and one in-pane header; studies remain available on demand. The shell uses 38 px top and 39 px bottom strips, 52 px left and 45 px right rails, 4 px gutters, a 6.1 px opening pitch and a 65 px right margin. Regular and extended sessions and dividend adjustment derive from retained history. Ranges fit displayed timestamps, including replay and narrow windows; date navigation selects an available session. Chart timezone choices update both axis formatting and the bottom clock.
- The export dialog stays open to show success with the file path or an error, and prevents duplicate requests while an export is pending. The demo reports file write results and offers PNG and CSV downloads in the browser. Image exports capture a frame after closing the dialog and report the result in a notice.
- Drawing magnet mode snaps placed anchors and dragged handles to nearby OHLC and visible overlay-study values, keeping each value paired with its bar. Targets follow the current pan, zoom and price scale within `DrawingManagerOptions::magnet_distance` (15 logical points by default). Hidden overlays and the drawing being dragged are excluded; undo and redo retain the snapped data coordinates.
- Drawing rail icons render on their 24 px grid with simpler Fibonacci, pattern, brush, magnet and keep-drawing details. The cursor family gains a pointer-and-halo icon for demonstration mode.
- Drawing rail selection uses a neutral fill, distinct from the magnet's contrasting latched fill. It keeps a 24 px icon grid on a 38 px pitch, with 35 px state fills and primary text ink. Its rules are 36 px wide and light-theme rules use a neutral grey. Drawing templates remain available in the Hide flyout instead of taking a utility row. Keep Drawing shows a pencil and lock, and the visibility menu uses an open eye. The right widget bar defaults to 45 px with 28 px icon grids; hosts should size its outer panel from `WidgetBarConfig::width`. Migration: `TemplatesButton::show` is removed; use the visibility flyout, or render `TemplatesButton::show_submenu` in a host menu.
- `UiExt::error_label`, `warning_label`, `success_label` and `info_label` use the active theme's error, warning, success and info colours instead of fixed colours, so the export dialog's error message reads at 4.5:1 or better in every preset.
- The interval and chart type menus close on Escape and on a click anywhere outside them, take the theme's menu frame, and end on a whole row above the window's edge with a scroll bar shown at rest when they scroll, so every interval and chart type can be reached. Interval codes no longer run into their descriptions, favourite intervals show a star icon instead of an asterisk and the current chart type a check icon instead of a "v", and every row reports its name, role and selected state to accessibility tools, shows a focus ring and scrolls into view when the arrow keys reach it. ([#289](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/289))
- The crosshair of Renko, Kagi, Line Break, Point and Figure and range bar charts writes times to the precision of the bars they are built from, instead of milliseconds for the bricks of one bar or the date alone for elements days apart, and the tooltip and the OHLC header leave out volume on chart types without it. A view of a single bar takes the precision from the bar before it instead of writing seconds.
- Renko, Kagi, Line Break, Point and Figure and range bar charts are drawn on a time scale of their own. Each brick, line, column or range bar takes one slot at the bar spacing and carries the time of the last bar that built it, so the time axis, the crosshair, the tooltip and the legend describe the element under the pointer. Panning, zooming, following the live edge and fitting the price scale work on the elements, and a few elements sit at the right of the pane as bars do instead of packed against its left edge. A change of chart type keeps the time at the right edge of the view, and a view at the live edge stays there. The time scale starts at the oldest kept element, and a chart with a feed stops asking for older history once older bars would not be drawn.
- Intraday time axis marks fall on multiples of their interval from midnight UTC, so each midnight shows its date, clock times appear between dates and panning keeps each remaining mark's time.
- Cypher labels C as an extension of XA and D as a retracement of XC, both from anchor prices, instead of reusing the XABCD ratios and omitting C. B keeps its retracement of XA.
- A multi-line study emits `IndicatorValue::Multiple` with one value per line, NaN for a line without a value, on every bar where any line has one, and never `Single`, as `IndicatorValue` now documents. TRIX, KST, SMI, RVI, PPO and PVO emitted `Single` with their main line while the signal warmed up, which every renderer drew on all lines, and Fisher Transform, Ehlers Fisher and Center of Gravity did so whenever the trigger or signal was exactly 0, as on a flat series. Their first value has no trigger or signal, since there is no previous value; a later trigger or signal of 0 is drawn. Migration: code matching `Single` on these studies reads the first value of `Multiple`; values do not change except the first trigger or signal, which is NaN instead of 0.
- Multi-line studies draw every line they compute. `Indicator::line_cnt` defaults to the number of `line_names` instead of 1, so TRIX, KST, SMI, RVI, Center of Gravity, Fisher Transform and Ehlers Fisher draw their signal or trigger line, Elder Ray its Bear Power, Vortex its VI-, Price Channel its lower and middle lines, Pivot Points its six support and resistance levels and Squeeze Momentum its squeeze flag (1 or 0) in its squeeze-on gray, which becomes its second colour ahead of the negative-momentum red. DMI adds the ADX line of the same length, equal to the `ADX` study's, after +DI and -DI. SuperTrend and Parabolic SAR are drawn as an up and a down trend line in their bullish and bearish colours. The main chart breaks a line at a NaN value as at a bar without one, and draws a run of one point as a dot; pane renderers skip a NaN value. Migration: an `Indicator` implementation that returns more `line_names` than lines overrides `line_cnt`. `SuperTrend` and `ParabolicSAR` output `Multiple(vec![up, down])`, with NaN on the line of the other trend, instead of `Multiple(vec![value, direction])`, and name their lines `Up Trend` and `Down Trend`, without the parameters; `DirectionalMovement` outputs `Multiple(vec![plus_di, minus_di, adx])`, NaN for a line without a value, and reports three lines and colours.
- `FloatingSelectionToolbar::show` no longer panics when the window is narrower or shorter than the toolbar. On an axis where it does not fit, the toolbar is placed at the window's left or top edge.
- The indicator dialog starts each study from its own default parameters instead of one length of 14 shared by all, so Bollinger Bands open at 20 and EMA at 12, and it edits the Keltner Channels ATR length and multiplier and the Stochastic %K smoothing, which it fixed at 10, 2.0 and 3. `IndicatorParams` gains `keltner_atr`, `keltner_multiplier` and `stoch_smooth`; struct literals must set them.
- Price auto-scale fits the visible values of visible overlay studies, every line they draw, together with the visible bars and through the same price transform, and skips values that are not finite. It fitted the bars alone, so a moving average or band outside their range ran off the pane. `Chart::set_indicator_autoscale(id, false)` leaves a study out of the fit without hiding it. `VolProfileVisible` and `VolProfileFixed`, whose values are volumes, draw no line. Migration: leave a study out to keep the bars-only range; `ChartState::fit_price_scale` takes the studies to fit, `std::iter::empty()` for the bars alone.
- The time axis does a bounded amount of work for very long time spans and large right offsets, and formats only the labels it shows. Spans of a few hundred years and more show evenly spaced years.
- TPO profiles add each bar in constant time instead of per row it spans, and a steady frame reuses the profiles of sessions whose bars did not change.
- A Heikin-Ashi chart no longer recomputes its whole history when the forming bar ticks or a new bar opens: it updates the last bars of the cached transform, and draws the visible bars without copying them.
- `TimeScaleOptions::fix_left_edge` works: when set, panning, zooming and scrolling stop with the first bar at the left edge of the chart, and when every bar fits the empty space is on the right. It had no effect, so its default is now `false`, matching how charts already behaved: the view can move up to 100 bars past the first bar.
- Dragging the chart pans the price axis vertically while auto-scale is off, so a range zoomed by hand can be moved to other prices. Dragging used to pan in time only.
- Price zoom stops at a span of a millionth of the price level on every price scale mode, also for views that cross zero: a percentage view, a logarithmic view of prices near 1, or a spread. It used to take a millionth of the visible bounds, which shrinks with each step across zero, until every price landed on the middle of the pane. Box zoom stops at the same span, and flat data on a logarithmic scale is fitted like flat prices, with margins taken from 1% of the price instead of 1% of its logarithm.
- Box zoom zooms in time: the bars inside the box fill the chart width, up to `TimeScaleOptions::max_bar_spacing`, centred on the box, and a box inside one bar shows that bar across the width. It used to re-centre the view on the box and keep the bar spacing.
- `DrawingPropertiesDialog` sizes its default frame to its tallest tab and uses the kit footer, with Apply as the only filled button and Delete and Clone as icon buttons, the kit colour picker, and line width swatches with a 1 to 10 px stepper; labels are in sentence case. Escape cancels the dialog and Enter applies it unless an open popup, colour picker or focused field takes the key.
- Percent changes have the sign of the price change when the reference price is negative, as in spreads and some futures: the data window, the symbol header, the floating and tracking tooltips, the OHLC header, the position tools, the forecast tool, the measure tools, the percentage price axis labels and `Bar::change_percent` divide by the magnitude of the reference price, so a rise from -10 to -5 reads +50%. A zero or non-finite reference gives 0%, so the tooltips no longer show an infinite or undefined percentage for an open of 0. Migration: `Bar::change_percent`, `OhlcData::change_percent` and `DataWindowInfo::change_percent` change sign for a negative reference price.
- Renko, Kagi, Line Break, Point and Figure and range bar charts are built once over the whole history and updated with each new bar, so their bricks, lines and columns no longer change while panning or zooming, and a steady frame no longer recomputes them. Only the newest 10,000 bricks or lines, or 5,000 columns or range bars, are kept.
- Hollow candles are coloured by the close versus the previous close and filled by the close versus the open; the baseline chart uses a configurable level (a price or a share of the pane height, 50% by default) and splits segments where they cross it; the HLC area fills above the close in the up colour and below it in the down colour; volume candles scale by the largest visible volume whether or not the volume pane is shown.
- Renko, Kagi, Point and Figure, range bars and Line Break use their own configured size, reversal and line count exactly, at any price level; a size the chart cannot use is reported on the chart instead of being replaced. Point and Figure columns draw each box at its own price instead of a running sum that drifted and stopped after 500 boxes, and a column whose boxes are under 2 px tall is drawn as a line.
- The time axis no longer panics when a bar is stamped near the end of the representable date range, such as a millisecond timestamp read as seconds or `DateTime::<Utc>::MAX_UTC`: times it would extrapolate past the range are left out, and `TickMarkGenerator::generate_marks` stops at the last mark that fits.
- The alert dialog and the alerts panel accept only a finite price or threshold, a multiplier above 0 and a lookback of at least one bar, keep Create disabled until the form is valid, and the dialog names the field to fix; an indicator alert set to compare with a threshold no longer loses an unreadable one.
- A magnifier tooltip with a zoom of 0, a NaN or infinite zoom, or a lens larger than the plot no longer freezes or garbles the chart on hover: the zoom is drawn within 0.1 to 100 (NaN as 0.1), the lens is at most as large as the plot's smaller side, and only the bars on screen are drawn in it.
- `model::to_range_bars_from_ticks` and `model::to_range_bars_from_ohlc` leave out a volume that would make a range bar's volume overflow, so two volumes near `f64::MAX` no longer give a range bar an infinite volume that breaks the volume scale.
- The keyboard shortcuts dialog draws each key over its badge instead of under it, sizes itself to its content up to the window height with a scroll bar shown at rest once the list scrolls, lists only the shortcuts the crate handles, adds that the chart keys need the chart focused, and closes on an Escape only when no popup is open and no text field has focus, consuming the key.
- The go to date picker no longer ignores a date it cannot parse: the field is outlined in the error colour, the line under it gives the expected format, and Go is disabled. In custom range mode Go is disabled until both ends are picked, the line under the field says which end to pick and then shows the range, a range no longer survives switching the mode or reopening the picker, and a typed date and the Today button pick a range end as a click on a day does. ([#525](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/525))
- The go to date picker closes when Go or Cancel is pressed. Go moved the chart and left the picker open over it, and Cancel did nothing; only the close button closed it. ([#524](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/524))
- `IndicatorDialog::with_config` keeps the configuration a caller sets: `show` no longer replaces it with the theme's on every frame. Each colour left `Color32::TRANSPARENT`, as in `IndicatorDialogConfig::default()`, follows the theme passed to `show`. `IndicatorDialogConfig::from_theme` sets every colour, so a configuration built from it no longer follows a theme change, and it now takes `muted_color` from `text_secondary` instead of `text_muted` and `sel_color` from `selection_bg` instead of `accent`.
- The indicator dialog's Community category and its Profiles and Patterns tabs list nothing instead of the built-in indicators, since no source supplies those entries yet. Community also hides indicators a host passes to `with_indicators`, as `IndicatorInfo` has no community flag.
- Returning to the live edge (a new bar while following it, tracking mode, the jump-to-latest button and `TimeScale::jump_to_latest`) restores the configured `TimeScaleOptions::right_offset`, or `right_offset_pixels` at the current bar spacing when set, instead of a fixed 2.5 bars; a time axis double-click restores the configured bar spacing before the offset, and a fixed right edge clamps it.
- Overlay study lines walk only the values of the visible bars each frame instead of every value from the first bar, so their drawing cost follows the visible bars, not the length of the history (about 26.3M to 4.3M instructions per frame for SMA and EMA on 1,000,000 bars).
- Volume bubbles draw as one mesh, each circle with as many sides as its radius needs to stay within a quarter of a pixel of round, instead of a filled circle and an outline shape per bubble, and the mesh reuses its memory from frame to frame. With 100,000 trades over 1,667 one-minute bars at 40 price levels a bar and 200 bars in view on a 1440 by 900 window, a frame tessellates 0.49 million vertices instead of 1.85 million and takes 13 ms instead of 33 ms, tessellation included; `cargo bench --bench frame_time -- bubbles` measures it. ([#694](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/694))
- Drawings that paint within a known reach of their anchors, such as shapes, patterns, Elliott waves and lines that are not extended, are skipped when that reach lies outside the pane's time range, and the manager tracks last frame's paints in time linear in the drawings instead of quadratic. The sine line draws its wave as one path instead of 200 line segments, and the circles of every drawing, such as anchor dots, wave labels and Fibonacci circles, become polygons with as many sides as their radius needs to stay within a quarter of a pixel of round instead of 32 to 128. With 500 drawings, every tool in turn, on a chart of 10,000 bars, a frame takes 15 to 17 ms instead of 17 to 21 ms when all are in view, at 364,000 vertices instead of 463,000, and 3.6 to 4.2 ms instead of 5.2 to 8.7 ms when they are spread across the history; `cargo bench --bench frame_time -- "500 drawings"` measures it. ([#155](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/155))
- A drawing that paints the same geometry as on the frame before reuses that frame's tessellation: each run of lines, paths and fills becomes one mesh, tessellated once and kept until the drawing, the view, the scale or the tessellation options change, while its text and small discs are painted as before. Drawings are now also skipped when their reach lies above or below the pane, except tools that paint at prices from the bars or run a leg on past an anchor. Line segments that continue one another become one path for every line style, not only dashed ones, which halves the vertices of arcs drawn as chained segments, and the half circles of time cycles and speed resistance arcs get as many sides as their radius needs to stay within a quarter of a pixel of round instead of 32. A Fibonacci wedge tessellates 2,088 vertices instead of 3,600 and speed resistance arcs 424 instead of 1,652. With 500 drawings, every tool in turn, all in view on a chart of 10,000 bars, a frame tessellates 327,000 vertices instead of 362,000 and takes about 20 ms instead of 26 ms on a loaded machine, tessellation 12 ms instead of 19 ms; `cargo bench --bench frame_time -- "500 drawings"` measures it. ([#155](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/155))
- `to_renko_bricks`, `to_kagi_lines`, `to_pnf_columns` and `to_range_bars_from_ohlc` build with the size they are given, at any price level, instead of raising it to a share of the price range of the bars (a 200th, a 500th for Kagi and Point and Figure) or to 0.0001, and range bars no longer stop splitting a bar after 100 steps. A size that is not a positive finite number, or a Point and Figure reversal of 0 boxes, gives no elements. Renko and range bars start from a later bar when the bars move by more than two million sizes. Migration: pass the size to draw; the former behaviour was `size.max((max_high - min_low) / 200.0)` for Renko and range bars and `/ 500.0` for Kagi and Point and Figure.
- A Line Break reversal line now starts at the open of the line it reverses, as the construction defines, instead of at its close.
- `ui_kit::Card` is visible: it fills with the theme's secondary surface, has a 1 px border and pads its content by 8 px. It filled with the panel colour and had no edge.
- `ui_kit::section::Section` draws its icon 8 px before the title, inside the header row, instead of left of the header where it was clipped. Section titles are 11 px.
- `ui_kit::tab_bar::TabBar` draws 12 px tabs 16 px apart from the content edge, each on a 28 px hit row, over a full-width rule that carries the active tab's underline, and leaves 8 px before the content. A hovered tab brightens, tabs report their selected state to accessibility tools and show a focus ring. The alert and drawing properties dialogs no longer draw a second rule under their tabs.
- `ui_kit::toolbar::ButtonGroup` lays out its content along the parent's direction, side by side in a horizontal toolbar and stacked in a vertical one, and draws its separators across the group, not down the rest of the parent.
- `ui_kit::sidebar_layout::SidebarLayout` insets its content 16 px from the divider and the top. Every `SidebarTab` reserves the icon slot, so labels line up whether or not a tab has an icon, and tabs report their selected state to accessibility tools and show a focus ring.
- `ui_kit::trading_labels::PnlLabel` puts the sign before the currency symbol and groups thousands ("-$98.10", "+$1,234.56"), and shows no sign for zero, including `-0.0`. `PnlLabel` and `SideLabel` use the theme's success and error colours, which are readable in every preset, instead of fixed chart tokens. Text that shows zero is in the normal text colour, not the gain or loss colour. The `PnlLabel::percent` example takes percent units, as the method does.
- The command palette draws each command's icon, description and shortcut, fills the selected and hovered rows with the theme's selection and hover colours, reports rows as selectable buttons to accessibility tools, and takes the theme's window corner radius and shadow.
- `ui_kit::PanelHeader` uses one title style, the theme's title size in the strong text colour, on a 36 px row with its actions 4 px apart. `PanelHeader::strong` has no effect and is kept for source compatibility.
- `CommandPalette::add_command` and `remove_command`, and the same `CommandPaletteHandler` methods, rebuild the listed commands and keep the highlight on the same command, so changing the commands while the palette is open no longer panics or makes Enter run the command next to the highlighted one.
- `AutocompleteEdit` no longer panics on Enter or Tab after a suggestion was picked from further down the list or the suggestions shrank: the highlighted row stays within the shown suggestions and returns to the first row after a pick.
- Renko, Kagi, Line Break, Point and Figure and range bar charts keep their newest bricks, lines, columns and bars when they reach their element limit, and draw the newest ones, so the chart always ends at the last bar instead of losing the most recent data on a long or volatile history.
- VWAP restarts at each trading day by default, counted in UTC, instead of accumulating over all loaded history. `VolumeWeightedAvgPrice::with_time_zone` and `with_session` count days in the instrument's zone and trading hours, so a session that crosses midnight in the exchange zone or in UTC stays one period, and a window that spans midnight starts the next trading day at its open. A bar of a day or longer, which a feed stamps inside its UTC period, counts on its UTC date; bars count as a day or longer when no two consecutive bars share a UTC date. `with_anchor` restarts at each week or month instead (`VwapAnchor`), and `with_std_dev_bands` adds upper and lower bands at multiples of the volume-weighted standard deviation of the typical price. Migration: `with_session_reset` is removed, since restarting at each session is now the default; values of `new()` and the factory's VWAP that were cumulative now restart each day, and those of `with_session_reset` restart in the exchange zone once a zone is set. To keep a cumulative VWAP over the whole series, use `AnchoredVWAP::new(0)`.
- The indicators, Go to, compare symbol, symbol search and export dialogs keep their content size under egui 0.37, which counts a window's frame and title bar in its size, and their title bars span the dialog. The indicators, Go to and compare symbol dialogs keep their titles centred over the dialog, which egui 0.37 centres in the space left of the close button. The date picker centres its calendar in its configured width, which the calendar overflowed because it left out the spacing between days, and the indicator dialog keeps its width when the configuration panel opens.
- An application that follows the system appearance, as egui does by default, keeps the theme applied with `apply_to_egui` when the system switches between light and dark. `apply_to_egui` wrote only the egui style in use, so egui switched to its own default style for the other appearance while the crate's widgets kept the theme's colours; it now styles both.
- KDJ smooths K and D recursively from 50, weighting the new value by `1 / k_smooth` and `1 / d_smooth` (a third by default), as the formula is published, instead of with simple averages of those lengths, starts on bar `period - 1`, and draws its D and J lines. A bar with a non-finite close or window range has no value, and K and D carry over it unchanged. Migration: K, D and J differ on every bar, J most, so thresholds tuned on the old values need retuning.
- Trend Intensity Index follows Pee's definition: the share of the size of the last `period` deviations from the `2 * period` bar simple moving average that lies above it, with the first value on bar `3 * period - 2`. It counted the bars above the average of the same window, which reads 50 on any straight trend. Flat closes read 50 at any price level. Migration: values change, and bars `period - 1` to `3 * period - 3` no longer have a value, so levels tuned on the old output need retuning.
- VW-MACD is the 12-bar minus the 26-bar volume-weighted moving average of the close, with a 9-bar EMA signal, and starts on bar 25. It took exponential averages of one cumulative VWAP from the first bar, which shrank toward zero as history grew. Migration: values differ from those of the cumulative average, and bars before the slow period have no value instead of a line, so thresholds tuned on the old line need retuning.
- `SnapService::snap_point` no longer panics when a snap target or the point is NaN: NaN targets are never chosen and a NaN coordinate is returned unchanged. `BarData::min_price`, `max_price`, `price_range` and `max_volume` skip NaN and infinite values, so the result no longer depends on bar order, and they return `None` when no finite value remains.
- Logarithmic, percentage, indexed-to-100 and inverted price scales reach the screen. Candles and every other chart type, indicators, markers, drawings, the crosshair, the tooltip, the grid and the price axis render through the chart's price scale; on an inverted scale High-Low bars are drawn, hollow candles keep their body clear of the wick, above-bar and below-bar markers sit outside the bar on screen, a click on a candle's body or wick selects the series, and the volume profile drawings draw their rows and box with the VAH and VAL labels outside the value area. The series settings dialog's scale mode and invert options apply to the main price scale (`Chart::apply_series_settings`), as does the mode given to `ChartBuilder::with_right_price_scale`. A new mode returns the scale to auto-scaling and clears the zoom history. Percentage and indexed modes are relative to the first visible close and keep the price order when that close is negative; the axis, the last-price label and the crosshair label show signed percentages or index values, and box zoom fills the pane with the selected prices in every mode. A range scaled by hand keeps its prices while the chart scrolls and when the zoom history restores it. A narrow logarithmic range is labelled in even price steps, and axis labels keep the same decimals at zero.
- Logarithmic, percentage, indexed-to-100 and inverted price scales reach the screen. Candles and every other chart type, indicators, markers, drawings, the crosshair, the tooltip, the grid and the price axis render through the chart's price scale, and the series settings dialog's scale mode and invert options apply to the main price scale (`Chart::apply_series_settings`), as does the mode given to `ChartBuilder::with_right_price_scale`. A new mode returns the scale to auto-scaling and clears the zoom history. Percentage and indexed modes are relative to the first visible close and keep the price order when that close is negative; the axis, the last-price label and the crosshair label show signed percentages or index values, and box zoom fills the pane with the selected prices in every mode. A range scaled by hand keeps its prices while the chart scrolls and when the zoom history restores it. A narrow logarithmic range is labelled in even price steps, and axis labels keep the same decimals at zero.
- The head and shoulders neckline runs through the two troughs instead of the two shoulders, and the target is projected from it at the last anchor: the head's distance from the neckline, measured at the head, beyond the neckline on the side away from the head. No neckline or target is drawn while the troughs share a bar, where the neckline would be vertical.
- XABCD, Cypher and ABCD ratio labels are measured in price, so they no longer change with zoom, pane size or scale mode. The XAB label is B's retracement of XA (`|B - A| / |A - X|`), where it showed `|B - X| / |A - X|`, and the ABCD label is `|D - C| / |B - A|` in price, where it compared on-screen leg lengths mixing time and price. A ratio whose base leg is flat is not shown, where it showed 0.
- Dragging the price axis zooms by each frame's movement around the point where the drag began, so the zoom no longer depends on how many frames the drag spans.
- The last-price line and label follow the newest bar instead of the last visible one, and are hidden while that price is off the scale.
- KAMA's efficiency ratio covers the last `period` bar-to-bar price changes, as Kaufman defines it, instead of the last `period - 1`, in both the net change and the sum of absolute changes. Migration: KAMA values from bar `period` onward differ on most series, so levels and signals tuned on the previous values need checking.
- Chandelier Exit measures its distance from Wilder's average true range, as the ATR study does, instead of a simple average of true range, and draws the short exit line as well as the long one. Migration: values after the first one change wherever the true range varies, so stops placed on the old values move.
- On Balance Volume starts at 0 on the first bar instead of at that bar's volume, which offset every value by that volume. Migration: every OBV value is lower by the first bar's volume, so thresholds set on the OBV level need the same shift; changes between bars are unaffected.
- Zig Zag draws its swings: each value between two consecutive pivots lies on the straight line joining them, instead of only the pivot bars having a value, which left nothing to draw. Bars before the first pivot and after the last stay empty, and a series without a reversal of at least the deviation has no values. Migration: pivots are the first and last bars with a value and the bars where the line changes direction, rather than the only bars with a value.
- Built-in studies clamp float parameters into a documented range at construction, NaN to the bottom of it, as they raise a length of 0 to 1: multipliers, percentages and costs to 0 or more, the Accumulation Swing limit move and the Ease of Movement and Market Facilitation divisors to `f64::EPSILON` or more, the ALMA offset to 0 to 1 and sigma to 0.001 to 100, and the Parabolic SAR factors to at most 1, with the maximum no lower than the start. `AccumulationSwingIndex::new(0.0)`, a NaN or negative band multiplier and an infinite ALMA sigma no longer turn every value into NaN or infinity.
- `FisherTransform` follows Ehlers' published construction: the normalised price is weighted by 0.33 instead of 0.66 before smoothing, the clamped value feeds the next bar, values beyond 0.99 are clamped to 0.999, and each Fisher value adds half of the previous one. Its output was off by up to several units in trends.
- Ichimoku plots Senkou Span A and B `displacement` bars after the bar they are computed on and the Chikou Span `displacement` bars before its close, instead of all on the same bar, and each line starts once it has a value instead of every line starting at the Senkou B period with 0.0 in place of missing values. Values remain one per bar, so the leading spans computed on the last `displacement` bars are not drawn. Migration: the value at bar `i` now holds the spans computed on bar `i - displacement` and the close of bar `i + displacement`; a line without a value at a bar is NaN.
- `EaseOfMovement` divides volume by 10,000 by default, as Arms defines the box ratio with volume in units of 10,000, instead of 1,000,000, which made every value 100 times too large. Migration: values with the default divisor are 100 times smaller; levels tuned on the old scale need dividing by 100, or `with_divisor(1_000_000.0)` keeps the previous scale.
- Light theme text and control outlines are readable: secondary and muted text reach at least 4.5:1 on panels and windows, unchecked checkboxes and radios, text inputs and combo boxes have a 3:1 outline, and egui's weak text uses the muted colour instead of fading by alpha. Dark theme secondary and muted text are raised to the same bar.
- `apply_to_egui` sets `error_fg_color` and `warn_fg_color` from the theme, so error text no longer uses egui's default pure red, and the status colours (success, warning, error, info) are defined per theme with at least 4.5:1 on its panels.
- Hover and pressed fills in the light theme are visible against white (1.2:1 and 1.3:1, from 1.1:1), and the accent hover and pressed shades and the danger button reds keep white text at 4.5:1 or better.
- The crate builds warning-free on current stable Rust: float literals that relied on the deprecated `f32` inference fallback are now explicitly typed.
- The documentation examples across the crate use the current crate name and module paths, and they compile as doctests with all features; those not marked `no_run` also run, and none is ignored. The `icon_from_path!` documentation now says its path is relative to the `assets/icons/` directory of the crate that invokes the macro.
- Cloning a `CustomIndicator` or `CustomTimeFormatter` now keeps its closure instead of replacing it with an empty or default one. Migration: `CustomIndicator::new` takes the closure directly (`CustomIndicator::new(name, |bars| ...)` instead of `Box::new(...)`), and the closure must be `Sync`.
- The indicator dialog's Momentum entry creates a new `Momentum` study (the close minus the close n bars earlier, in price units) instead of Rate of Change; `Momentum` is also registered in the indicator factory.
- The export dialog no longer offers actions it cannot perform: the save path is typed into an editable field instead of a Browse button that set a fixed name, the data export text lists only the bar fields, and `ExportDialog::with_formats` limits the offered formats to those the host supports. `Exportable::copy_to_clipboard`, which discarded the image and reported success, is removed; copy a rendered `egui::ColorImage` with `egui::Context::copy_image` instead. The demo copies the chart image to the clipboard and saves PNG exports to the chosen path.
- Dashed lines are clipped to the visible area before they are dashed, so a drawing anchored far off screen no longer hangs the UI thread or exhausts memory. Every dashed line in the crate (drawings, crosshair, price lines, session breaks, indicator pane zones) now shares this routine.
- Auto-scaling flat price or indicator data now centres the series in a range proportional to the price level instead of collapsing to a near-zero range.
- The anchored VWAP drawing computes the volume-weighted average of the typical price from its anchor bar, with bands one and two volume-weighted standard deviations away.
- The fixed range and anchored volume profiles are built from bar volume, spreading each bar's volume over its high-low range, with the point of control and a 70% value area. The anchored volume profile is placed with a single click and runs to the latest bar.
- The regression trend fits the closes between its anchors by least squares and shows the resulting R² and the Pearson correlation of the closes.
- The anchored VWAP, the volume profiles, the regression trend and Heikin-Ashi candles recompute when a bar inside their span is corrected, instead of showing values from the old bars until an anchor moved or the span grew. `ChartState::set_data` compares the new bars with the current ones to tell a correction from an append or a last-bar update, one pass over the bars per call.
- A live volume profile or regression trend no longer recomputes its whole span when the forming bar ticks or a new bar opens, or after another drawing was dragged: it adds that bar to the cached result for the bars before it (a profile only while the bar trades inside the profile's price range). At 1,000,000 bars a new bar costs about 0.002 ms instead of 20 ms for a profile, and a tick 0.001 ms instead of 5 ms for a regression trend. Heikin-Ashi candles no longer copy the whole transformed series on every frame.
- Long and short position tools have a stop of their own that moves independently of the target, so risk/reward reflects the levels set; P&L replays the bars after the entry and shows the position as closed once the stop or the target trades. Both read n/a while the stop or the target is on the wrong side of the entry.
- The forecast drawing shows the predicted change over its bar span and resolves to success once a bar reaches the target by the target bar, or to failure once the target bar has closed without that.
- The bars pattern projection draws shifted copies of the selected bars.
- The chart watermark falls back to the chart symbol and is hidden when there is none, instead of showing a placeholder.
- The OHLC header of a chart without a symbol prints prices with the same magnitude-based decimals as the legend instead of 8 decimals, and labels the last bar's volume as volume instead of 24h volume. The top toolbar's symbol pill shows no symbol instead of AAPL when none is set, the chart and series context menus leave out their alert and order items until there is a symbol, and `SymbolHeader` leaves out empty info fields instead of drawing bare separators.
- Zooming the price axis in by hand stops at a span of one millionth of the price level instead of narrowing to a few nanounits, and repeated zooming out can no longer overflow the price range to infinite or NaN bounds.
- `Timeframe::duration_ms` saturates at `i64::MAX` for custom timeframes too large to represent instead of overflowing into a panic or a negative duration, and parsing a custom timeframe whose second count overflows is rejected.
- Built-in studies raise a length of 0 to 1 and accept any longer length, alone or mixed with short ones, so a zero, huge or swapped period no longer panics, exhausts memory or yields only NaN (for example `KAMA::new(0)`, `SmoothedMA::new(0)` and `MACross::new(50, 20)`).
- SMA, VWMA, LSMA, Bollinger Bands, RSI, MACD and VW-MACD return one `None` per bar instead of no values when there are fewer bars than their period.
- Stochastic no longer panics when all its periods are 1, Volume Profile Fixed Range no longer panics on a bar with a NaN price, and Rank Correlation no longer panics sorting NaN closes.
- Rainbow Oscillator keeps a running sum instead of every SMA level, so a huge level count no longer exhausts memory.
- Rainbow Oscillator divides the close's distance from the mean of its averages by the close's range over the last `levels` bars, as published, instead of by the mean itself. A bar whose range is zero has no value. Migration: every value changes scale, from a percentage of the price level to a percentage of the recent range of the close, so levels set on the previous values need to be set again.
- Volume profiles refuse a row count above 10,000 and yield `None` for every bar instead of allocating that many rows for each bar.
- `to_tpo_profiles` widens the tick size to a whole number of ticks when the bars span more than 1,000 ticks and skips bars with a non-finite or inverted range, so one wide or infinite bar no longer hangs the chart or exhausts memory. Migration: `TPOProfile` has a `tick_size` field holding the row height used, which `TPOProfile::new` takes as a second argument; pass it to `tpo_count_at`, `price_levels`, `letters_at` and `classify_shape`.
- The CVD documentation states that it is estimated from bars and uses no trade or order book data.
- The keyboard focus ring uses the applied theme's `ui.focus` colour (egui's selection stroke when no theme has been applied) instead of the selection fill at reduced alpha, so it reaches 3:1 on panels and windows in every preset, and 4.5:1 in the high-contrast preset (it was 1.2:1 in light themes and 2.0 to 2.3:1 in dark ones).
- Widgets that meant the accent read the theme accent instead of the selection fill, which is pale in light themes: tab underlines, the sync button and its checkboxes, the selected drawing colour swatch, the open colour picker, the layout name input, the date picker range and Primary list item buttons. Text on an accent fill uses the theme's on-accent colour, and Danger list item buttons fill from the danger button token.
- Selected sidebar items and active chart control toggles use the selection fill and text colour (the sidebar painted accent text on an accent tint, 2.4:1 in dark themes), active tab labels use the strong text colour, descriptions on selected timeframe, chart type and template rows are no longer faded to 3.7:1, and the success status message uses the theme's success colour instead of the accent.
- Selected rows, tabs, menu items and calendar days render readable text on the selection fill instead of an empty accent bar (the selection text colour equalled the fill): white on the accent in dark themes, accent text on a pale accent fill in light themes.
- Tooltips, popups and menus have padding, a hairline border, an elevated fill, a small radius and a tight shadow; windows get padding and a border.
- `apply_to_egui` sets item spacing, window padding and menu padding from the design tokens instead of zero, so labels no longer touch their controls; toolbars, dropdown rows and stacked chart panes keep their own explicit gaps.
- Switching the theme repaints everything: the bottom toolbar, date picker, range switcher, timeframe bar, right panel tabs, symbol header, chart context menus, OHLC tooltip, replay bar and command palette resolve their colours from the active theme instead of keeping the dark theme they were built with. Fields a caller sets on the public `config` of `TimeframeToolbar`, `TimeframeBar` or `RightPanelTabs` are kept; only the fields left at their theme-derived value follow the theme.
- Custom-painted buttons (top toolbar, drawing toolbar, widget bar and the `ui_kit` buttons) draw a focus ring when they have keyboard focus.
- The dialog close button no longer draws an accent outline at rest; it looks like an icon button, so only keyboard focus draws a ring around it.
- The command palette search icon follows the theme instead of staying white, so it is visible in the light theme.
- The native demo starts on Linux: it enables wgpu's default backends instead of none.
- The web demo publishes, next to its page, the licences of the egui default fonts (Hack, Ubuntu Light and the egui icon subset of emoji-icon-font) and the Lucide icons that its WebAssembly module embeds; `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` lists the fonts.
- The README, package description, crate docs and ROADMAP state what the code does: the number of drawing tool types (counting cursor modes) and of registered indicator names, with a unit test that keeps those counts in step with the code; no unproven icon count; and no claim of magnet snapping, drawings saved by the chart or templates saved to disk, none of which works yet. The crate and module docs describe the modules, presets and data contracts as they are: which builder sets what, where pan and zoom live, that `fetch_historical` needs `supports_historical`, and that nothing consumes `Datafeed` or the services yet.
- ROADMAP ticks an item only when it works end to end: unwired widgets, scale modes the chart does not render, drawing tools with wrong geometry and indicator lines that are computed but not drawn are now unticked or carry their limits.
- The README's Rust snippets compile and run as doctests against the current API (including the `Datafeed` example), and the install lines use version `0.2`.
- The `QuoteSource` docs describe when it is actually called: nothing in the crate polls quote sources.
- Bar replay no longer panics when a frame is due more bars than remain, and `ReplayState::update` returns the number of bars actually advanced.
- `ListItem` allocates its row once: rows are 28 px apart with no gap, hover, pressed and selected fill the whole row, and every content type starts its icon and text 8 px inside the row. Custom `ListItemContent` implementations paint inside `ContentContext::rect` and return the row response instead of allocating space of their own. Row colours come from the active theme, with a pressed state distinct from hover and on-accent text on selected rows, and rows report their role, label and selection to accessibility tools.
- The `AutocompleteEdit` popup is exactly as wide as its input, has the popup border, aligns its suggestions with the input text and draws them in the theme's text colour.
- `FormRow` takes the height of its content instead of the remaining height of the panel, so consecutive rows and a following `FormGrid` stay visible. The label is centred on the widget's line, and the description sits below it in the theme's muted text colour.
- The `FilterWidget` search icon and clear button sit inside the field, 8 px from its edges, and the field no longer grows by a pixel when hovered or focused. Clicking the clear button now clears the query.
- The `ColorPicker` swatch is 28 px with a border that stays visible on dark colours and brightens on hover. Its popup is as wide as the swatch grid and uses the popup border, and the row below the grid shows the hex value and opacity of the selected colour.
- The compare symbols dialog draws its symbols, labels and background in the theme's colours when `CompareSymbolsConfig` leaves them at the default instead of drawing them transparent.
- The `ui_kit` buttons share one state model. Rest, hover and press are each a visible step: neutral fills move one step further from the surface, and Primary and Danger fills darken, so pressed no longer equals hover or looks paler than it; a selected `Button`, `IconButton` or `SplitButton` is drawn as Primary, filled with the accent; `enabled(false)` renders exactly like a button inside `add_enabled_ui(false)`; and a disabled Primary or Danger button is drawn as Secondary, so its faded label stays as readable as other disabled buttons, with an accent outline on a disabled Primary `Button` so a selected one still reads as selected.
- `ButtonVariant` colours follow the active theme: Primary is the theme accent with its hover and pressed shades and on-accent text, Secondary a neutral fill one step above the surface, Outlined a border in the secondary text colour, Ghost the primary text colour, and Danger the danger button reds with on-accent text.
- `Button` is as wide as its laid-out label plus 12 px on each side, instead of an estimate from the byte length, so every label gets the same padding and labels with multi-byte characters fit.
- `SplitButton` points its chevron down whether or not it is expanded.
- The `ui_kit` buttons report their role, label (the tooltip for icon-only buttons), and enabled and selected state to accessibility tools, and show their tooltip while disabled.
- The kit `Modal`, `ConfirmDialog`, `PromptDialog` and the command palette draw their dimming backdrop below their window instead of over it, so the dialog is shown at full contrast. Clicking the backdrop closes a closable modal.
- `PromptDialog` keeps the text typed into it between frames instead of resetting it to the initial value, so Enter and the submit button return what was typed.
- The data window prints a negative price with the decimals of the positive price of the same magnitude, so -37.63 reads `-37.63` instead of `-37.63000000`.
- Saved drawings round-trip: `stored_to_drawing` restores the anchors (`chart_points`), so a restored drawing no longer disappears on the next frame, every tool type loads back, and every setting (line style, fill, font, extend, arrows, lock, visibility, per-timeframe visibility, z-order, Fibonacci levels, volume profile, regression trend and position size) is kept.
- Every drawing tool's display name parses back to that tool with `FromStr`, ignoring case; the Fibonacci extension, time zone, speed resistance fan, trend-based time and speed resistance arcs tools and the five Elliott wave tools were not parsed.
- A drawing whose tool type is unknown is skipped with the error `unknown drawing tool "Name"` instead of one listing all 95 tool types.
- `ColorType::from_hex` returns an error for input that is not six or eight hexadecimal digits, instead of panicking on a multi-byte character such as `"aÄÄb"` or accepting a sign such as `"+F+F+F"`.
- `ColorType::from_hex` and `ColorType::from_rgba` read the alpha as unmultiplied, as CSS and `Color32::from_hex` do, so `"#FF000000"` is fully transparent and `"#FF000080"` is half transparent red. They stored the unmultiplied channels as premultiplied, which drew a transparent colour as additive red and a translucent one too bright. Migration: code that passed premultiplied channels to `from_rgba` builds `ColorType::Solid(Color32::from_rgba_premultiplied(r, g, b, a))` instead. Colours saved by earlier versions keep the values they were saved with; build them again from their hex or RGBA source to get the corrected colour.
- Ease of Movement leaves a bar with zero volume and a nonzero range out of its average instead of counting it as a bar that did not move. A bar with a NaN or infinite high, low or volume has no value, and a window without any bar with a raw value has no value.
- Zig Zag confirms its first pivot from the first reversal instead of starting at an extreme of the first bars, extends the current leg on an outside bar instead of reading it as a reversal, and skips a bar whose prices are not finite or not positive. The rules are documented on `ZigZag`. Migration: values change on most series, since the line now starts at the first confirmed pivot, usually earlier than before, pivots around outside bars move, a series at or below zero, such as a spread, has no values, and a deviation of 0 gives no values instead of a pivot on almost every bar, so swing levels read from the previous values need checking.
- Fibonacci retracement levels are measured back from the second anchor, the end of the move: level 0 is drawn on the second anchor and 100% on the first, with each level's price label, background band and hit area at the same height, and the Reverse option, which had no effect, now swaps the two anchors. Migration: saved retracements draw with their levels flipped; set `FibonacciConfig::reverse` to keep the previous placement.
- The Gann fan draws each angle at its labelled ratio: an NxM line has N/M times the slope of the 1x1 line, so 8x1 is the steepest and 1x8 the flattest. They were drawn the other way round, so the drawn lines did not match the lines a click selects, and 1x3 used 0.333 instead of one third.
- The sine line passes through both anchors, a peak and a trough half a period apart, with the centre guide midway between them and the amplitude label at half their price distance. It crossed the centre line at the first anchor and returned to it at the second, so it missed the second anchor unless both had the same price. Migration: a saved sine line keeps its anchors, and its first anchor is now a peak or trough instead of a crossing of the centre line.
- The parallel channel's second line starts and ends on the bars of the first two anchors, straight above or below them, and passes through the third anchor. It was shifted along the base line by a construction at right angles to it, so its ends sat on other bars and the connecting lines were slanted; the hit test now uses the drawn line.
- The Gann box draws its 2x1, 3x1 and 4x1 lines and their mirrors from the first anchor to 1/2, 1/3 and 1/4 of the far edges, and counts its 0%, 50% and 100% levels from the first anchor's price. The lines started at the top-left corner whatever corner the box was drawn from, and ended at a pixel slope that matched the labels only for a square box.
- Time cycles repeat the interval between their anchors forward in time: each cycle is a half circle as wide as that interval, standing on the first anchor's price, from the earlier anchor to the right edge of the chart. They drew six concentric half circles around the first anchor, from 0.5 to 3 times the anchor distance.
- Several Fibonacci tools are corrected: channel levels lie on the side of the base line where the third anchor is, where they were always drawn on one side; speed resistance arcs open towards the first anchor's price, below the end of a rise and above the end of a fall, where they were always drawn below; the speed resistance fan extends towards the side of its second anchor, where a fan drawn right to left was mirrored to the right; time zones fall on 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8 and on along the Fibonacci sequence, where the line at 1 was drawn twice; and the spiral is a golden spiral through the second anchor whose radius grows by the golden ratio every quarter turn, where it grew by that ratio per full turn from a tenth of the anchor distance and ignored the second anchor's direction.
- `to_renko_bricks` reverses only after a two-brick move: a close must move one brick beyond the open of the last brick, and the reversal brick starts at that open. A one-brick move against the trend drew a reversal brick over the last brick. Renko charts now show fewer reversal bricks.
- `parse_date_range("MTD")` starts at 00:00 UTC on the first day of the current month, as `YTD` starts on the first day of the year, instead of 30 days before now. `1M` still covers the last 30 days.
- `parse_date_range` rejects a relative count below 1, such as `"last -5 days"` or `"last 0 days"`, with `DateRangeParseError::InvalidNumber` instead of returning a reversed or empty range, and returns the same error for a count too large to represent instead of panicking.
- An absolute range from `parse_date_range` includes its end date: `"2024-01-01 to 2024-12-31"` ends at the start of 1 January 2025, and a range whose two dates are equal covers that day instead of being refused. An end date on the last day chrono can represent returns `InvalidDateFormat`. Migration: the end of an absolute range is one day later than before, so code that added a day to include the end date no longer needs to. The end is exclusive: keep a bar when its time is before the end. `DateRangeParseError::InvalidDateOrder` reads "End date must not be before start date".
- TPO profiles count each period once at each price row instead of once per bar, and a bar's period follows the whole minutes elapsed since the session's first bar instead of advancing by at most one period per bar, so a gap leaves its periods empty and a bar 95 minutes into a profile with 30-minute periods gets letter D instead of B. On bars shorter than the period, a row touched by several bars of one period holds one letter and one TPO, which changes the point of control, the value area, single prints and poor highs and lows. On bars longer than the period, each bar takes the letter of the period it opens in, so letters skip the periods it also spans, for example A, C, E on 1-hour bars with 30-minute periods. Building a profile walks each row once per period instead of once per bar. Migration: `TPOLetter::ts` is the open time of the first bar of the letter's period instead of the bar that placed it; letters and counts differ from earlier versions on series whose bars are shorter than the period or have gaps, and letters differ on bars longer than the period.
- `to_tpo_profiles` uses the default letters when `TPOConfig::custom_letters` is an empty string, instead of panicking with a remainder by zero.
- Time scale options whose minimum bar spacing exceeds the maximum, or is NaN or infinite, no longer panic when the chart is built, zoomed or box zoomed: a minimum above the maximum counts as the maximum, a NaN or infinite minimum as the default 0.5, a negative minimum as 0, and a NaN or infinite maximum as unlimited.
- The crosshair price label sits in the price axis, aligned with the tick labels and in their font, shows the price with the decimals of the axis ticks instead of eight, and no longer ends in a "+". The time label sits on the time axis instead of over the volume bars, in the axis date format with the year when it is not the current one, and the vertical line runs through the volume pane.
- The crosshair's time label and its magnet snap take the bar nearest the pointer, the one the OHLC readout shows; they took the bar to its left when the pointer was past the middle between two bars.
- The last price tag is aligned with the tick labels and in their font, and its text is black or white, whichever reads better on the candle colour; it was light grey at 2:1 or less. An axis label under the last price tag or a crosshair label is left out instead of showing in part, and the crosshair price label covers the last price tag it overlaps.
- The crosshair line and labels follow the theme (`chart.crosshair_line`, `chart.crosshair_label_bg` and `chart.crosshair_label_text`); the light and high contrast themes showed the dark theme's. The `CrosshairOptions` colours, including the label colours that nothing read, default to `Color32::PLACEHOLDER`, the theme's colour, and override it when set. The light theme's crosshair line is darker, 3.2:1 on white.
- The floating tooltip shows its labels and values in two aligned columns in the proportional font, prices with the legend's decimals (at most `price_precision`), and a 1 px border in the theme's border colour instead of a 2 px ring in the candle colour. It sits in the top corner of the price pane away from the pointer instead of next to it, so it no longer covers the hovered bar. `TooltipOptions::border_color_bullish` and `border_color_bearish` default to `Color32::PLACEHOLDER`, the theme's border colour; the tracking bar and the magnifier take the candle, crosshair and text colours from the theme.
- Price and time axis labels use one font size, so month and day labels share a baseline.
- The selected drawing's handles are round, an accent ring around the chart background like the selection dots of a series, instead of white squares with a dark outline; the handle being dragged is filled with the accent. `HandleConfig::color` and `selected_color` default to `Color32::PLACEHOLDER.to_array()`, the theme's accent.
- A new drawing is pink (`DrawingManagerOptions::default_color`), a colour no default study uses, instead of a blue close to the EMA's.
- The legend's O, H, L and C labels and the bullet after the symbol use the theme's secondary axis text colour instead of fixed greys ([#459](https://github.com/userFRM/egui-charts/issues/459)), and its values and change the theme's success and error colours instead of the candle colours, which read at 3:1 on the light chart.
- The chart, series and drawing context menus hide the crosshair, its labels and the tooltip while they are open, open 2 px from the pointer (the drawing menu 6 px, clear of the clicked handle) and flip to the other side of it to stay 8 px inside the window, size themselves from the laid-out text and elide labels wider than 300 px, and draw with the theme's menu frame, hover fill and shadow. Rows show the bound shortcut, formatted for the platform ("Ctrl+R" on Linux and Windows), only for actions with a key binding; the crate handles only Delete, destructive rows ("Remove 5 indicators", "Delete") use the danger colour, both menus format the clicked price with thousands separators, and every row reports its label and role to accessibility tools.
- `NotificationPanel` stacks its toasts 8 px apart, newest nearest the corner, 16 px from the window corner. Every toast was drawn at the same spot, flush to the corner, so only one was visible.
- Toasts are opaque and follow the theme: the window fill, border and popup shadow, text in the theme's text colour (a title in the strong text colour, its message in the secondary text colour), and the icon and progress bar in the theme's colour for the kind. They were translucent fixed-colour slabs, the same in every theme, with error text below 4.5:1. The close button can be clicked, and a timed toast's progress bar spans the toast's content instead of stopping 16 px short.
- `Alert` takes its colours from the theme: the icon in the theme's colour for the kind, the fill and border in that colour at 10 % and 40 %, and the title in the strong text colour with the message under it in the secondary text colour. It used fixed tokens tuned for the dark theme, so several icons fell below 3:1 in the light theme and the high contrast theme showed the dark colours. Every alert fills the available width, the dismiss button can be clicked, and the text starts 8 px after the icon instead of 16 px.
- `status_message` draws Info in the theme's info colour instead of the plain text colour, starts at the left edge of the content around it instead of 8 px in, and leaves 4 px above instead of 6 px.
- Acceleration Bands average each bar's widened high and low, as Headley defines them, instead of widening the average high and low by the average range, and draw all three bands.
- Klinger Oscillator weighs volume by Klinger's volume force, built from each bar's range and the cumulative range of the current trend, instead of by the trend sign alone, and draws its signal line. Equal sums of high, low and close keep the current trend, a series too short for the long average has no values instead of values from a partial average, and a bar with a non-finite price has no value without ending the study. Migration: values differ from the signed-volume averages computed before, and every value is `Multiple([oscillator, signal])`, the signal NaN until it exists, where it was `Single(oscillator)` until the signal existed.
- `BarData::to_csv`, `to_csv_string` and `to_csv_with_indicators` write a bar's fraction of a second, and `BarData::from_csv` reads it, so bars less than a second apart keep their times through a CSV file. Whole-second times are written as before.
- A replay speed that is not finite plays at 1x and a custom speed is clamped to 0.1x to 100x, so `ReplayAction::SetSpeed(f32::NAN)` or a negative speed no longer freezes a replay that still reports playing.
- `to_range_bars_from_ticks` closes one bar per full range a tick crosses, up to 100, and always returns the bar holding the last trade, so a tick that closes a bar no longer loses the rest of its move and the series ends at the last traded price. It keeps the newest 5,000 bars, like `to_range_bars_from_ohlc`.
- Trend Detection Index follows Pee's definition, the absolute n-bar sum of n-bar momentum minus the excess of the 2n-bar over the n-bar sum of absolute momentum, and starts on bar 3n - 1. It returned the net move minus the path length, which is never positive. Migration: levels tuned on the old values need resetting, and the first value now comes 2n - 1 bars later.
- The True Strength Index starts its averages at the first price change instead of a made-up zero change on the first bar, which skewed the index for dozens of bars after its warm-up, and draws its signal line.
- ADX starts from the mean of the first n DX values, as Wilder defines it, instead of n - 1 values divided by n, so it no longer reads low after its warm-up.
- ATR with length 1 has a value on a single bar, its range, instead of none until a second bar exists.
- The Commodity Selection Index computes Wilder's formula, ADXR * ATR(n) * (V / sqrt(M)) * (1 / (150 + C)) * 100, from the ADX and ATR studies, and starts on bar 3n - 1; it used a simple mean of true ranges, an unsmoothed DX and a division by the close. Migration: `CommoditySelectionIndex::new(period, cost)` is now `new(period)`; set the contract with `with_point_value`, `with_margin` and `with_commission`. The old cost parameter had no counterpart in Wilder's formula.
- The release workflow fails when `cargo publish` fails; it skips the publish only when crates.io already has the exact version.
- Bars with a non-finite value, a high below the open or close, a low above them or negative volume are dropped and counted instead of drawn; bars out of time order are sorted, and of several bars with one open time, or in one slot of the timeframe for a chart built with a feed, the last is kept. This holds for `Chart::new`, `set_data` and `update` and for the bars a feed sends.
- A live update no longer copies the history: the chart merges new bars in place and updates its studies once per frame from the first changed bar instead of recomputing them on every update.
- Removing a study no longer moves the selection to another study, and removing the selected study clears the selection.
- Loading older bars with `Chart::set_data` moves the drawings with their bars, and so does a bar that `Chart::update` or a feed inserts between stored bars.
- The Schiff pitchfork's median starts at the first anchor's time, halfway between the first and second anchors, and runs through the midpoint of the second and third anchors with the tines parallel to it. It started at the first anchor and was aimed halfway between that anchor and the midpoint, so the median and both tines had the wrong slope.
- An Image drawing draws its checkerboard only where it is visible, so a large frame, or a stored anchor far off screen, no longer stalls every frame.
- Fibonacci retracement and extension levels are drawn, labelled and hit at the price they are labelled with on every price scale, where on a logarithmic scale the line sat at the interpolation of the anchors' screen positions and its label named another price. The hit test of a retracement and of an extension follows the visible levels of their config, where the extension tested the retracement's fixed levels. `Drawing` gains the field `level_ys`, the screen y of each visible level, rebuilt by `update_screen_coords`. Migration: construct a `Drawing` with `Drawing::new` instead of a struct literal, and call `update_screen_coords` after changing `fib_config` or `chart_points` before rendering or a hit test. A retracement or extension with fewer than two `chart_points`, as `Drawing::add_point` leaves it, now draws nothing, where it drew its levels from the screen points.
